## 0. THE VERDICT LINE I AM ASKING FOR

    VERDICT: CLOSES | DOES NOT CLOSE -- <one line naming the deciding item>

## 1. WHAT SHIPPED, AND WHAT THIS PASS IS FOR

`#530` removed a terminating call from `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)` in `src/devices/dev_lca.c`
(the DEC 21066 "LCA" chipset's sparse ISA I/O window on the Alpha `alphabook1` machine).  The old
code computed `ofs = relative_addr & 3` and, when `ofs > len`, printed a message and ended the
emulator process -- so an ordinary guest BYTE access to an ISA port that is 2 or 3 mod 4 ended
the emulator.  The fix, and its 352-row detector `regress/lca_isalane_probe.py`, went through six
review passes before this one.

**Closing pass 6's flagship ruling (2026-09-17) found the FIX sound and found ONE detector gap:**
a width-conditioned variant, `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && len <= 8)`, went undetected by all
352 rows, because that probe is driven by guest INSTRUCTIONS and the widest Alpha store (STQ) is
8 bytes -- no instruction-driven row can express `len > 8`.  The ruling pre-committed the
disposition before anything was measured:

> *"escapes AND len>8 is guest-reachable -> L2, add a len>8 row in-round"* ... *"`&byte, len`
> diverges WITHIN the probed width set (catchable, so a contract line suffices); codex's
> `len<=8` diverges only OUTSIDE it (uncatchable, so it cannot be discharged that way)."*

`len > 8` was shown guest-reachable (section 4), so a row was built: **`regress/lca_widewrite_probe.py`
-- THE SUBJECT OF THIS PASS.**  It shipped in 8bd97e4 together with the fix.

**Scope, per this project's repair-chain rule:** review EFFORT is on the new row; the FLOOR
question -- *is there a variant, within the fix's own dependency set, that no shipped row
distinguishes from the shipped code?* -- is asked of the COMPOSITE detector (both probes).

## 2. THE RULES THIS PASS APPLIES (inlined, because four seats cannot read them: glm, deepseek, minimax, and kimi-k3:cloud when it stands in for the kimi CLI)

* **Floor clause (d) -- the repair must be observable.**  A detector must distinguish the shipped
  fix from a variant that KEEPS the guard but nulls its effect: a predicate weakened or qualified
  (`if (X)` -> `if (X && Y)`), a mask narrowed, a bound relaxed.
* **Its bound (DEPENDS-ON).**  The variants enumerated are (1) the predicates DOMINATING the diff's
  edited lines, walked up only to the ENCLOSING FUNCTION BOUNDARY, and (2) operand swaps drawn only
  from lvalues the guarded BODY reads.
* **Joint cells (adopted 2026-09-04).**  *"DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands
  and the body-read lvalues EXACTLY WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM.  For each
  lvalue take the PARTITION the body's own tests induce ... A value or combination the body never
  tests as a case is DISCLOSED under clause (e)."*
* **Clause (e) -- the contract.**  The probe header names which variants it is designed to catch;
  a variant outside the stated contract is DISCLOSED debt, not a breach.
* **L2** = a floor breach, repaired in-round however late it is found.  **L4** = a wrong record
  (prose claiming coverage a measurement refutes), corrected in place.
* **Class closure.**  *"When two consecutive passes find breaches of one class, the next repair owes
  the CLASS closure: enumerate the full space from the function's own inputs, close every cell in
  ONE diff, and write into the contract which cells are covered and which are deliberately not."*

**Note the tension I see, and please rule on it rather than on my reading of it:** the body of
`lca_isa` tests `len` only against 0.  By the joint-cells letter, a variant keyed on `len > 8` (or
`len > 16`) introduces a partition the body never tests -- clause (e).  Pass 6's ruling instead
made `len <= 8` an L2 on the ground that it diverges only OUTSIDE the probed set.  Both cannot be
the whole rule, and question Q2 below turns on which governs.

## 3. THE ENCLOSING FUNCTION, WHOLE, AS COMMITTED (`8bd97e4:src/devices/dev_lca.c`)

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
{
	unsigned int ofs, i;
	uint8_t byte;

	relative_addr >>= 5;

	/*
	 *  #530: the byte for ISA port P sits at buffer position (P & 3) % len
	 *  on BOTH arms -- the lane for the 21066's own len 4/8 accesses, and
	 *  the rule the write arm already applied wherever the old guard let
	 *  it run.  That guard's `ofs > len' exit(1) ended the host on a guest
	 *  byte access to a port 2 or 3 mod 4 or a word access to a port 3 mod
	 *  4 (ungated BWX; and at a port 3 mod 4 also the 1- or 2-byte head
	 *  of an unaligned load or store that memory_rw splits at 1 KB),
	 *  and the read arm discarded the
	 *  fetched byte at ofs == len.
	 *  One byte is forwarded whatever len is (the size field a<4:3> is
	 *  not decoded here).  len 0 has no producer; refuse it before the %.
	 */
	if (len == 0)
		return 0;
	ofs = (relative_addr & 3) % len;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
		byte = data[ofs];
		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
	}

	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);

	for (i=0; i<len; i++)
		data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;

	return 1;
}
```

The diff (`git show --format= 8bd97e4 -- src/devices/dev_lca.c`), comment lines elided:

```diff
-	ofs = relative_addr & 3;
-	if (ofs > len) {
-		fatal("[ ofs=%i len=%i in lca_isa access function. "
-		    "aborting ]\n", ofs, len);
-		exit(1);
-	}
+	if (len == 0)
+		return 0;
+	ofs = (relative_addr & 3) % len;
 
 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
-		byte = data[ofs % len];
+		byte = data[ofs];
```

**`relative_addr` on entry** is the offset into the 2 MB sparse window `lca_isa` is registered over
(`memory_device_register(..., "lca_isa", LCA_PCI_SIO, 0x10000 << 5, ...)`, `LCA_PCI_SIO` =
`0x1c0000000`).  After `>>= 5` it is the ISA PORT number P; the discarded low five bits (call them
k) are never read.  `LCA_ISA_BASE` is where the real ISA devices are registered.

## 4. THE ONLY PRODUCER OF `len > 8` I FOUND (inlined, `8bd97e4`)

`src/core/memory.c`:

```c
void store_buf(struct cpu *cpu, uint64_t addr, const char *s, size_t len)
{
	size_t psize = 1024;	/*  1024 256 64 16 4 1  */

	while (len != 0) {
		if ((addr & (psize-1)) == 0) {
			while (len >= psize) {
				cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr,
				    (unsigned char *)s, psize, MEM_WRITE,
				    CACHE_DATA);
				addr += psize;
				s += psize;
				len -= psize;
			}
		}
		psize >>= 2;
	}

	while (len-- != 0)
		store_byte(cpu, addr++, *s++);
}
```

`src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c`, `alpha_prom_call()` -- reached by the guest instruction
`CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe` (the word the Alpha machine setup stores at the PROM entry point):

```c
	case PROM_R_GETENV:
		/*  a1 = variable id, a2 = char *buf, a3 = bufsize  */
		switch (a1) {
		...
		case PROM_E_BOOTED_FILE:
			s = cpu->machine->boot_kernel_filename;
			break;
		case PROM_E_BOOTED_OSFLAGS:
			s = cpu->machine->boot_string_argument;
			break;
		...
		}
		/*  Copy at most a3 bytes.  */
		len = a3;
		if (strlen(s) < len)
			len = strlen(s) + 1;
		store_buf(cpu, a2, s, len);
		break;
	...
	/*  Return from the PROM call.  */
	cpu->pc = cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_RA];
```

`a2` and `a3` are guest registers.  `boot_string_argument` comes from the `-o` command-line option
via `strdup(optarg)` (`src/core/main.c`) -- no length cap in the emulator.

`src/cpus/memory_rw.c`: non-PHYSICAL accesses are split at 1 KB (`#define MRW_SPLIT_MASK 0x3ff`;
the loop `while (len > granule - offset)`), and the device handler is then called with the
chunk's length unchanged (`res = mem->devices[i].f(cpu, mem, paddr, data, len, writeflag, ...)`).
On a READ, the dispatcher zeroes the caller's buffer before calling the device (`#95`:
`if (writeflag == MEM_READ) memset(data, 0, len);`).

**MY ENUMERATION OF THE WIDE CELLS THIS PRODUCER REACHES -- please WALK it, do not trust it.**  A
store_buf chunk of size C is issued only at an address aligned to C, and never crosses a 1 KB
boundary, so it reaches `lca_isa` whole.  With A = 32P + k (P = port, k = the five discarded bits):

    C = 16     k in {0, 16}, any P        lane = P & 3 in {0,1,2,3}      8 cells
    C = 64     k = 0, P even              lane in {0, 2}                 2 cells
    C = 256    k = 0, P % 8 == 0          lane = 0                       1 cell
    C = 1024   k = 0, P % 32 == 0         lane = 0                       1 cell
                                                                        -------
                                              WRITE direction only:    12 cells

C = 64/256/1024 also need a string of at least C - 1 characters (so `len >= C`), which `-o` can
supply.  **The row covers ONE of the twelve: (C=16, k=0, lane 3).**  No READ-direction wide
producer was found (store_buf only writes; PROM PUTS reads one byte at a time).

## 5. THE NEW ROW (`8bd97e4:regress/lca_widewrite_probe.py`)

Its contract, verbatim from the file's header:

```
CONTRACT (clause e).  DESIGNED TO CATCH: any guard that declines a len > 8 WRITE to lca_isa, in either
the guard form or the write-arm-conjunct form.  DISCLOSED, NOT CAUGHT: (i) which of the 16 bytes
reaches the port -- only s[3] does, and the other 15 are dropped; that is `lcawidefold', whose reopen
condition ("a guest found issuing GETENV into the ISA window") THIS ROW MEETS; (ii) the READ side --
store_buf only writes, and no wide-read producer was found, so a read twin would drive a path no guest
reaches; (iii) widths 64/256/1024 -- 16 was measured; the others share the same `len > 8' predicate.
```

The session, CONDENSED -- helper definitions and comments elided at `...`; every line shown is
verbatim (the machine is booted `-E alpha -e alphabook1 -M 64 -o ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOP`
with a dummy kernel; commands go to the emulator's debugger over a pty; `step N` executes N guest
instructions; `print t4` reads a register):

```python
BOOTSTR = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOP"          # strlen 16, so len = min(a3=16, 17) = 16
ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
A0, A1, A2, A3 = 16, 17, 18, 19
CALL_PAL_PROM = (0x00 << 26) | 0x3fffffe
SENT = 0x0bad1d8a                             # pre-seed: a faulting load leaves it
K0SEG       = 0xfffffc0000000000
def k0(p):  return K0SEG | p
LCA_PCI_SIO = 0x1c0000000
def SP(port): return LCA_PCI_SIO + (port << 5)
SCRATCH, CODE = 0x01020000, 0x01040000

SCR, PRESET = 0x2ff, 0x5a                     # the ns16550 scratch register (COM2 base 0x2f8 + 7)
WANT = ord(BOOTSTR[(SCR & 3) % 16])           # s[3] == 'D' == 0x44 -- derived, not typed in
RA = 26                                       # Alpha ra = r26
p1 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + load_const(T1, PRESET) + [STB(T1, 0, T2)]           # preset the SCR
p2 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T2)]                         # CONTROL: reads back
p3 = (load_const(A0, 0x22) + load_const(A1, 0x8) + [LDQ(A2, 0, T0)]
      + load_const(A3, 16) + [LDQ(RA, 8, T0), CALL_PAL_PROM])               # GETENV, 16-byte store
p4 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T2)]                         # the SCR afterwards
RET = k0(CODE) + 4 * (len(load_const(T0, SCRATCH)) + len(p1) + len(p2) + len(p3))
consts = [k0(SP(SCR)), RET]                   # slot 0: the SCR; slot 8: where the PROM call returns
phases = [(p1, None), (p2, "t3"), (p3, None), (p4, "t4")]
...
row("wide alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive", "the guest survives an inline CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe")
row("wide live", t3, PRESET, "LIVENESS + DEVICE SIGNATURE: a 1-byte store round-trips through the SCR")
row("wide getenv", t4, WANT, "a 16-byte store_buf write lands s[3] on the SCR; M2 (`&& len <= 8') leaves the PRESET")
```

(`0x2ff << 5 = 0x5fe0`: not 1024-, 256- or 64-aligned, 16-aligned -- so exactly one 16-byte chunk.
A session whose prints do not all arrive is retried once with tripled patience.)

**Measured** -- predictions written to a file BEFORE any run, every value matched, and the shipped
file re-run as shipped with identical scores:

    base                            3/3   wide getenv = 0x44 ('D')
    `&& len <= 8` (write arm)       2/3   wide getenv = 0x5a (the preset: the store fell into the READ arm)
    `len == 0 || len > 8` (guard)   2/3   wide getenv = 0x5a (the preset: refused)

The committed `dev_lca.c` is md5-identical (`8084ff07...`) to the source that run measured.

**Settled by an earlier run, disclosed rather than hidden:** the row's FIRST construction executed
the `CALL_PAL` without loading `ra`; `alpha_prom_call` returns THROUGH `ra`, so the call returned
to address 0 and every build read 0 alike.  The pre-run predictions file had said in advance that a
base short of 3/3 means the ROW is broken, so it was read that way, and `RET` is now computed from
the instruction layout.

The PARENT probe's header now says (verbatim, `8bd97e4:regress/lca_isalane_probe.py`):

```
#  *** DISCHARGED 2026-09-18 -- THE ROW NOW EXISTS, IN ITS OWN FILE: regress/lca_widewrite_probe.py. ***
#  It drives the non-instruction path this header names -- a PROM GETENV (CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe) whose
#  a2 is the ns16550 SCR -- so store_buf issues ONE 16-byte memory_rw into lca_isa.  Measured, every
#  value predicted before any run (artifacts/lcalenzerodrop/widewrite_20260918/PRETABLE.out):
#      base 3/3 (SCR <- s[3] = 'D');  `&& len <= 8' 2/3;  `len == 0 || len > 8' 2/3
#  Both mutants that swept THIS file's 352 rows clean are killed there.  Kept in a SEPARATE file on
#  purpose: folding it in here would move 352 measured rows and owe a full re-measure to show nothing
#  shifted, for no gain in coverage.  The width cell is therefore COVERED, not merely disclosed -- but
#  by that file, so an edit that deletes it re-opens this cell and this header says where to look.
```

## 6. SETTLED PREMISES (so nobody spends an answer re-deriving them)

* **The fix is sound** -- pass 6's flagship ruling, *"The fix, the L4 grading, and the m_lenzero
  disposition are all sound -- the gap is the detector's width coverage, not the repair."*
* **`len == 0` has no producer** -- store_buf bottoms out at 1-byte accesses, and a CPU access is
  1..8 bytes; measured EQUIVALENT (37/37).  (`memory_rw`'s truncation only clips `len` DOWN and is
  NOT the warrant -- three seats showed that on pass 6.)
* **`len > 8` reaches `lca_isa` whole** -- section 4, and measured: the row's base leg shows the
  16-byte write arriving.
* **For every `len >= 4`, `(P & 3) % len == P & 3`**, so the shipped WRITE arm forwards
  `data[P & 3]` for every width from 4 to 1024.  It forwards ONE byte whatever the width; the other
  bytes of a wide store are dropped -- that is a separate reopened row (`lcawidefold`) and is NOT
  this pass's question.
* **The parent's 352 rows are all instruction-driven, `len <= 8`.**  So any variant that is
  identical to the shipped code for `len <= 8` scores 352/352 on the parent BY CONSTRUCTION.

## 7. QUESTIONS

**Q1 -- CHECK THESE CANDIDATE WEAKENINGS.**  Each is a single edit to the function in section 3.
For each, say whether it is **EQUIVALENT** to the shipped code, or **DISTINGUISHED** by a shipped row
(name the row), or **NOT DISTINGUISHED** -- and give the FIRST input (width, k, port/lane,
direction) at which it and the shipped code differ.  My predictions are in the last column;
they are derivations, not measurements, so check them.

    id   the edit                                                         my prediction
    (a)  `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {`
           -> `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && len <= 16) {`                  NOT distinguished; differs at C=64
    (b)  `if (len == 0)` -> `if (len == 0 || len > 16)`                    NOT distinguished; differs at C=64
    (c)  `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {`
           -> `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && (len <= 8 || (relative_addr & 3) == 3)) {`
                                                                            NOT distinguished; differs at C=16, lane 0
    (d)  `ofs = (relative_addr & 3) % len;`
           -> `ofs = (relative_addr & 3) % (len > 8 ? 8 : len);`           EQUIVALENT everywhere
    (e)  `if (len == 0)`
           -> `if (len == 0 || (len > 8 && relative_addr < 0x200))`        NOT distinguished; differs at C=16, port < 0x200
    (f)  `byte = data[ofs];` -> `byte = data[len > 8 ? 0 : ofs];`           DISTINGUISHED by `wide getenv` (0x41 vs 0x44)
    (g)  insert as the FIRST statement, before `relative_addr >>= 5;`:
           `if (len > 8 && (relative_addr & 0x10)) return 0;`               NOT distinguished; differs at C=16, k=16
    (h)  `for (i=0; i<len; i++)` -> `for (i=0; i<len && i<8; i++)`          EQUIVALENT (the #95 pre-zero; and no wide read)

**Q2 -- THE RULE QUESTION, and it decides whether this pass closes.**  For every variant you find
NOT distinguished AND reachable: is it a **floor breach (L2)** that owes rows in-round, or
**clause-(e) debt** that owes only an honest contract?  Name the rule you apply -- pass 6's
*"diverges only outside the probed set"* criterion, or the joint-cells letter (*values the body never
tests are disclosed*) -- and say why the other does not govern.  Then:

* if ANY is L2: pass 6 and this pass have found breaches of ONE class (width), so the
  **class-closure** amendment applies.  Is my twelve-cell enumeration in section 4 the right space
  to close?  What does it omit or over-count?  Which cells would one diff row, and which would the
  contract disclose, with what warrant?
* if NONE is L2: are the contract's clause (iii) (*"the others share the same `len > 8'
  predicate"*) and the parent header's *"The width cell is therefore COVERED, not merely disclosed"*
  **wrong records (L4)**?  I think the second overstates: it is true of the two variants that were
  measured and false of (a), (b), (c), (e), (g) if they are undetected.

**Q3 -- PREMISE AUDIT OF THE ROW ITSELF.**
* Does each row's observable actually vary under the variant it is meant to catch?  (`wide alive`
  looks survive-only to me -- name any variant under which it alone goes red, or say it has none.)
* Is `wide live` a real device-signature control -- could RAM, or a missing device, return 0x5a?
* Is `RET` correct, and would a variant that broke the PROM return be read as a construction
  failure rather than as a detection?  (Both variant legs printed t4 = 0x5a, i.e. the program
  reached phase 4.)
* **Is GETENV really the only producer of `len > 8` into this window on `alphabook1`?**  Could a
  PHYSICAL access -- which the 1 KB split exempts -- deliver a larger length (a device DMA into the
  sparse window)?  Name it, or say you checked and found none.

**Q4 -- OPTION-SET COMPLETENESS.**  For the width class, the dispositions I can see are: (1) one row
per reachable cell in one diff; (2) a representative subset plus an honest disclosure of the rest;
(3) disclosure only; (4) one GETENV session that sweeps every reachable (C, k, lane) cell with an
ANCHORED oracle (the forwarded byte names which string offset arrived).  Is there a member I did
not name -- grep the fork for how other wide-producer rows were handled -- and which would you
choose?

**Q5 -- If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE, say so.**

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""lca_widewrite_probe.py -- the width row #530 owed (lcalenzerodrop).  MEASURED 2026-09-18.

WHAT IT PINS.  `#530`'s instruction-driven detector, lca_isalane_probe.py, cannot express a store wider
than 8 bytes -- the widest Alpha store, STQ, is 8 bytes (its own header, :148-151).  So a guard that
declines only len > 8 swept all 352 of its rows: `#530`'s KILLLEN8 leg (`len == 0 || len > 8') and the
`&& len <= 8' write-arm conjunct both scored 352/352.  CLOSE6_VERDICT.md pre-committed the disposition:
*escapes AND len > 8 is guest-reachable -> L2, add a len > 8 row in-round.*  This is that row.

THE PATH -- the NON-INSTRUCTION one, because no instruction can reach it:
    CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe      the word machine_alpha.c:189 stores at PROM_ENTRY_PADDR;
                            cpu_alpha_palcode.c:519-520 dispatches it to alpha_prom_call
      a0 = 0x22             PROM_R_GETENV          (thirdparty/alpha_prom.h:69)
      a1 = 0x8              PROM_E_BOOTED_OSFLAGS  (:85) -> s = boot_string_argument, set by -o
      a2 = k0(SP(0x2ff))    the ns16550 SCR, 64-bit K0SEG, loaded via put d + LDQ
      a3 = 16               len = min(a3, strlen(s)+1) = min(16, 17) = 16   (palcode.c:141-143)
      ra = the next insn    alpha_prom_call RETURNS THROUGH ra (palcode.c:153) -- see RUN 1 below
    -> store_buf(cpu, a2, s, 16) -> ONE memory_rw(a2, 16): 0x5fe0 fails 1024/256/64 alignment and
       passes 16 (memory.c:839-852) -> lca_isa: port 0x2ff, ofs = (0x2ff & 3) % 16 = 3 -> s[3] = 'D'

MEASURED (regress/artifacts/lcalenzerodrop/widewrite_20260918/logs/TABLE.out; every value predicted
in PRETABLE.out at 14:59:49, BEFORE any run, and every value matched):
    base        3/3   wide getenv = 0x44 ('D')
    M2_len8     2/3   wide getenv = 0x5a (the PRESET: the store fell into the READ arm)
    KILLLEN8    2/3   wide getenv = 0x5a (the PRESET: `return 0' -> MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED, and there
                                          is no Alpha exception branch in memory_rw.c:603-624)

RUN 1 WAS AN L1 CONSTRUCTION FAILURE and is preserved, not hidden (logs/run1_L1_no_return_address/):
the CALL_PAL was executed inline WITHOUT loading ra, so the PROM call "returned" to address 0, phase 4
never ran, and every binary read 0 alike.  The PRETABLE had pre-committed that a base leg short of 3/3
means the ROW is broken, not the fix -- which is how it was read.

CONTRACT (clause e).  DESIGNED TO CATCH: any guard that declines a len > 8 WRITE to lca_isa, in either
the guard form or the write-arm-conjunct form.  DISCLOSED, NOT CAUGHT: (i) which of the 16 bytes
reaches the port -- only s[3] does, and the other 15 are dropped; that is `lcawidefold', whose reopen
condition ("a guest found issuing GETENV into the ISA window") THIS ROW MEETS; (ii) the READ side --
store_buf only writes, and no wide-read producer was found, so a read twin would drive a path no guest
reaches; (iii) widths 64/256/1024 -- 16 was measured; the others share the same `len > 8' predicate.

WITNESS vs DETECTOR.  This is a DETECTOR (green once the defect is gone).  #530's rung-3 WITNESS is
lca_isalane_probe.py's 13/16 on the committed build.
"""
import os, pty, re, select, sys, time

BIN     = sys.argv[1]
LABEL   = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
MACH    = "alphabook1"
BOOTSTR = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOP"          # strlen 16, so len = min(a3=16, 17) = 16
DUMMY   = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")   # as the parent

# ---- encoder, copied from lca_isalane_probe.py so the encodings are identical
ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
A0, A1, A2, A3 = 16, 17, 18, 19       # Alpha ABI a0..a3 = r16..r19.  NOT defined in the parent probe,
                                      # which deliberately names only T0..T6 -- defined here, on purpose.
def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
    assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
    return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)
def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
def LDBU(ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0a, ra, rb, disp)
def STB (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0e, ra, rb, disp)
def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
CALL_PAL_PROM = (0x00 << 26) | 0x3fffffe      # == 0x03fffffe, the word machine_alpha.c:189 stores

def load_const(reg, val):                     # 32-bit only, exactly as the parent
    lo = val & 0xffff
    hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
    if lo >= 0x8000:
        hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
    ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
    if lo:
        ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
    return ops

SENT = 0x0bad1d8a                             # the parent's pre-seed: a faulting load leaves it
def sent(reg): return load_const(reg, SENT)

K0SEG       = 0xfffffc0000000000
def k0(p):  return K0SEG | p
LCA_PCI_SIO = 0x1c0000000
def SP(port): return LCA_PCI_SIO + (port << 5)
SCRATCH, CODE = 0x01020000, 0x01040000

# ---- harness: the parent's session(), with ONE change -- `-o BOOTSTR' -- in a separate function so
#      the parent's 352 rows cannot be affected by it
def session(cmds, settle, spin):
    pid, fd = pty.fork()
    if pid == 0:
        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
                       "-o", BOOTSTR, "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
        os._exit(127)
    buf = ""
    def rd(t):
        nonlocal buf
        end = time.time() + t
        while time.time() < end:
            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
            if not r:
                continue
            try:
                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
                if not d:
                    return False
                buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
            except OSError:
                return False
        return True
    alive = rd(settle)
    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
        try:
            os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
        except OSError:
            alive = False; break
        if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.45):
            alive = False; break
    try:
        os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.5)
    except OSError:
        pass
    try:
        os.close(fd)
    except OSError:
        pass
    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive

def build(consts, phases):                    # copied verbatim in behaviour
    prog = load_const(T0, SCRATCH)
    lens = [len(prog)]
    for ops, _ in phases:
        prog += ops
        lens.append(len(ops))
    cmds  = ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 8 * i, v) for i, v in enumerate(consts)]
    cmds += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog)]
    cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % lens[0]]
    for (ops, pr), n in zip(phases, lens[1:]):
        cmds.append("step %d" % n)
        if pr is not None:
            cmds.append("print %s" % pr)
    return cmds

def printed(t, reg):
    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return None if not m else int(m.group(1), 16)

rows = []
def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-14s %-24s %-24s %-56s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok

# ---- the session
SCR, PRESET = 0x2ff, 0x5a
WANT = ord(BOOTSTR[(SCR & 3) % 16])          # s[3] == 'D' == 0x44 -- derived, not typed in
#  *** RUN 1 WAS AN L1 CONSTRUCTION FAILURE AND THIS IS ITS FIX. ***  alpha_prom_call ENDS WITH
#  `cpu->pc = cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_RA]' (cpu_alpha_palcode.c:153) -- the real PROM entry is reached
#  by `jsr', which loads ra.  Run 1 executed the CALL_PAL inline WITHOUT setting ra, so the call
#  "returned" to address 0, phase 4 never ran, and t4 kept its initial 0 on every binary alike
#  (logs/run1_L1_no_return_address/).  ra is now loaded with the address of the instruction AFTER the
#  CALL_PAL -- COMPUTED FROM THE LAYOUT, not typed in, so an edit to any phase cannot silently strand it.
RA = 26                                       # Alpha ra = r26
p1 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + load_const(T1, PRESET) + [STB(T1, 0, T2)]           # preset the SCR
p2 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T2)]                         # CONTROL: reads back
p3 = (load_const(A0, 0x22) + load_const(A1, 0x8) + [LDQ(A2, 0, T0)]
      + load_const(A3, 16) + [LDQ(RA, 8, T0), CALL_PAL_PROM])               # GETENV, 16-byte store
p4 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T2)]                         # the SCR afterwards
RET = k0(CODE) + 4 * (len(load_const(T0, SCRATCH)) + len(p1) + len(p2) + len(p3))
consts = [k0(SP(SCR)), RET]                   # slot 0: the SCR; slot 8: where the PROM call returns
phases = [(p1, None), (p2, "t3"), (p3, None), (p4, "t4")]

print("lca_widewrite -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s -o %s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH, BOOTSTR))
cmds = build(consts, phases)
txt, alive = session(cmds, 3.0, 4.0)
#  MIRRORS THE PARENT'S run(): a session whose prints did not all arrive TIMED OUT rather than
#  measured, and is retried ONCE with tripled patience -- "the machine may be loaded".  Without this,
#  a slow run under contention reads as a FAILED ROW: a phantom red that the row did not earn.
retried = False
if printed(txt, "t3") is None or printed(txt, "t4") is None:
    retried = True
    txt, alive = session(cmds, 3.0 * 3, 4.0 * 3)
print("WIDEWRITE_RETRIED=%s" % ("yes" if retried else "none"))
if os.environ.get("WIDEWRITE_RAW"):
    open(os.environ["WIDEWRITE_RAW"], "w", encoding="utf-8").write(txt)

t3, t4 = printed(txt, "t3"), printed(txt, "t4")
row("wide alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive",
    "the guest survives an inline CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe")
row("wide live", t3, PRESET,
    "LIVENESS + DEVICE SIGNATURE: a 1-byte store round-trips through the SCR")
row("wide getenv", t4, WANT,
    "a 16-byte store_buf write lands s[3] on the SCR; M2 (`&& len <= 8') leaves the PRESET")
ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
print("WIDEWRITE_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
print("WIDEWRITE_DONE")

[32m succeeded in 404ms:[0m

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "bus_isa.h"
#include "bus_pci.h"
#include "cpu.h"
#include "device.h"
#include "emul.h"
#include "interrupt.h"
#include "machine.h"
#include "memory.h"
#include "misc.h"

#include "thirdparty/alpha_lcareg.h"

#define	LCA_ISA_BASE	(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x10000000)
#define	LCA_ISA_MEMBASE	(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x18000000)


/*  #516: warn-once latch bits, one per stub.  PER-INSTANCE, reached through
    `interrupt->extra' -- see the comment on lca_interrupt_assert().  */
#define	LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT	0	/*  #516  */
#define	LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT	1	/*  #516  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_CONF_CFG1	2	/*  #518: IOC_CONF<1> set -- CFG AD<1:0> = 10/11, not a cycle type  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WMASK0	3	/*  #518: unsupported W_MASK0 encoding declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WMASK1	4	/*  #518: unsupported W_MASK1 encoding declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WEN		5	/*  #518: a DMA window enabled -- translation not modelled  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_DEF_W	6	/*  #518: default-arm write declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_DEF_R	7	/*  #518: default-arm read answered with zero  */

struct lca_data {
	struct pci_data		*pci_data;

	unsigned int		warned;		/*  #516  */

	uint64_t		ioc_conf;
	uint64_t		tlb_enable;
	uint64_t		window_base_0;
	uint64_t		window_mask_0;
	uint64_t		window_t_base_0;
	uint64_t		window_base_1;
	uint64_t		window_mask_1;
	uint64_t		window_t_base_1;
};


/*
 *  lca_interrupt_assert():
 *
 *  Line 0 = ISA interrupt.
 */
void lca_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) interrupt->extra;	/*  #516  */

	/*  #516: this was `fatal(); exit(1)' with NO GUARD -- not a refused value,
	    an unimplemented path -- and DEVINIT below installs it as the ISA
	    interrupt callback that `bus_isa_init' wires to BUS_ISA_IDE0|IDE1.  So an
	    ordinary ISA interrupt ended the host, needing no unusual value anywhere.
	    MEASURED on two independent guest paths: one store to the 8042 command
	    port (regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py, 3/4) and three byte stores to the
	    ns16550 on IRQ 4 (regress/lca_irqstub_uart_probe.py, 2/4).

	    *** THE STUB IS NOT REPLACED BY A FORWARD, BECAUSE A FORWARD WOULD BE A
	    NO-OP.  ALPHA HAS NO INTERRUPT DELIVERY AT ALL. ***  `cd.alpha.irq_asserted'
	    is written by `cpu_alpha.c' and read NOWHERE.  `cpu_dyntrans.c' turns a
	    pending interrupt into a delivered one for FIVE architectures -- ARM and
	    M88K and PPC through their own `irq_asserted', MIPS through the COP0
	    exception model, and SH through `int_to_assert' -- and ALPHA IS ABSENT
	    FROM THAT LIST.  (An earlier draft of this comment said "ARM, M88K and
	    PPC only": that enumeration was made by grepping for `irq_asserted', which
	    is a CORRELATE of delivering rather than the property, and it missed the
	    two architectures that deliver by another mechanism.  The Alpha conclusion
	    is unchanged and is strengthened -- it is the outlier among five, not
	    three.)  Calling `cpu_interrupt()' here would set
	    a field nothing consumes.  Implementing delivery is a SUBSYSTEM -- the
	    dyntrans dispatch, the PALcode entry and the IPL check -- and is its own
	    round, not this one.

	    So the disposition is DECLINE plus a bounded announce, and what it buys is
	    stated narrowly on purpose: THE HOST STOPS DYING.  Interrupts do not start
	    working; they were never delivered on this architecture and still are not.

	    The latch is PER-INSTANCE (`interrupt->extra'), not function-static: a
	    function-static bit would let one guest access to one device instance
	    silence the diagnostic for every other instance, which is a class already
	    filed against this tree.  */
	if (d != NULL && !(d->warned & (1u << LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT))) {	/*  #516  */
		d->warned |= 1u << LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT;
		fatal("[ lca: ISA interrupt assert not implemented (Alpha has no "
		    "interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\n");
	}
}


/*
 *  lca_interrupt_deassert():
 *
 *  Line 0 = ISA interrupt.
 */
void lca_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) interrupt->extra;	/*  #516  */

	/*  #516: the sibling of lca_interrupt_assert() above, same reasoning.  A
	    SEPARATE latch bit, so the two are distinguishable: a driver normally
	    reaches both within one interrupt cycle, and one shared bit would report
	    whichever arrived first and hide the other entirely.  */
	if (d != NULL && !(d->warned & (1u << LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT))) {	/*  #516  */
		d->warned |= 1u << LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT;
		fatal("[ lca: ISA interrupt deassert not implemented (Alpha has no "
		    "interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\n");
	}
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)
{
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	int tag, type1, bus, dev, func, reg;	/*  #515  */
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  1. Decompose the address into a tag.
	 *
	 *  According to NetBSD's lca_pci.c, the address is composed like this:
	 *
	 *	addr = tag << 5 | (regoffset & ~0x03) << 5 | 0x3 << 3
	 */
	reg = (relative_addr >> 5) & 0xfc;
	tag = (relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff;

	/*
	 *  2. Decompose the tag into bus, dev, and func.
	 *
	 *  The tag can be constructed in one of two ways. On the primary
	 *  bus (nr 0):
	 *
	 *	tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (function << 8);
	 *
	 *  and on other busses, the tag is a normal:
	 *
	 *	tag = (bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)
	 */
	/*  printf("tag = 0x%x\n", (int)tag);  */

	/*  #515: `bus' used to be BOTH the configuration CYCLE TYPE and the
	    DESTINATION BUS, and conflating them is the whole defect.  IOC_CONF bit 0
	    selects the cycle type only -- 21066/21066A HRM S6.2.9: "PCI address bits
	    ad<1:0> come directly from configuration cycle type register (IOC_CONF,
	    Section 6.4.2) bits <1:0>" -- and it does NOT carry a bus number.  The
	    destination bus of a type-1 cycle lives in the ADDRESS, per the PCI Local
	    Bus specification's type-1 layout (bus 23..16, dev 15..11, fn 10..8,
	    reg 7..2), which S6.2.9 defers to explicitly.

	    Before this, a guest that merely SET bit 0 -- a value the CONF guard
	    ACCEPTS -- made any subsequent access in this window reach
	    `fatal("TODO. Non-zero bus."); exit(1)'.  MEASURED: two ordinary guest
	    accesses, nothing refused anywhere on the path
	    (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py, 3/4 pre-fix; artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/).  */
	type1 = d->ioc_conf & 1;	/*  #515  */
	bus = 0;			/*  #515  */

	if (!type1) {			/*  #515  */
		/*  #511: was `0x800 << dev'.  `0x800' is a plain int (2^11) and `dev'
		    reaches 20, so the last iteration evaluates 2^31 -- a SIGNED
		    OVERFLOW, undefined by C99 6.5.7p4, not merely
		    implementation-defined.  MEASURED under UBSAN before the change:
		    "dev_lca.c:125:21: runtime error: left shift of 2048 by 20 places
		    cannot be represented in type 'int'", reached by an ordinary guest
		    ldq into the PCI config window (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py against
		    build-asan; artifacts/lcashiftub/ubsan_HEAD_20260907.raw).

		    `0x800U' makes the operand unsigned, so 2048U << 20 is 0x80000000U --
		    inside unsigned int -- and NOTHING ELSE MOVES.

		    *** THE REASON, CORRECTED: AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID `tag' IS "ALREADY A
		    WIDER UNSIGNED QUANTITY".  THAT IS FALSE -- `tag' is a plain signed
		    `int' (:92) -- AND FOUR PASS-2 SEATS CAUGHT IT.  Worse, the claim was
		    backwards: being WIDER is what would CREATE a difference, not remove
		    one.  Had `tag' been `uint64_t', the old `int' 0x80000000 is NEGATIVE
		    and would SIGN-EXTEND to 0xffffffff80000000 for the `&', while
		    `0x800U << 20' zero-extends to 0x0000000080000000 -- two different
		    masks.  The seats were right about the mechanism and it does not fire
		    here only because of the type.

		    WHAT ACTUALLY HOLDS: both operands are 32-bit, so the `&' is performed
		    at 32 bits either way -- `int & int' before, and after, `tag' converted
		    to `unsigned int' by the usual arithmetic conversions.  The RESULT'S
		    BIT PATTERN IS IDENTICAL; only its type changes, and it is consumed
		    solely as a truth test in `if (...)'.  So no guest-visible value moves,
		    and the repair is owed purely by the LANGUAGE. ***

		    *** THE LOOP THIS SITS IN IS THIS FILE'S OWN GRACEFUL-DEGRADATION
		    PRECEDENT -- the `dev >= 21' arm below has a commented-out
		    fatal()/exit(1) and substitutes `dev = 0' instead, and `lcabadval'
		    cites it as a transferable warrant.  The safe path contained the UB. ***  */
		for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)
			if (tag & (0x800U << dev))	/*  #511  */
				break;
		if (dev >= 21) {
			/*  fatal("[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\n");
			exit(1);  */
			dev = 0;
		}
	} else {
		/*  #515: a type-1 cycle carries its destination in the address.  */
		bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;	/*  #515  */
		dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;	/*  #515  */
	}

	func = (tag >> 8) & 7;
	/*  printf("bus=%i dev=%i func=%i reg=%i\n", bus,dev,func,reg);  */

	/*  #515: a type-1 cycle whose bus field is zero selects NOTHING.  Type-1 is
	    how a bridge is asked to reach a bus BEHIND it; the primary bus is reached
	    with type-0 cycles, whose IDSEL encoding is decoded above.  Forwarding this
	    case would set cur_bus = 0 and hand the access to whichever primary-bus
	    device matched -- `i82378zb' is registered at 0:7:0 by DEVINIT below -- so
	    simply decoding `bus' and stopping there would replace a host kill with a
	    NEW MISROUTING.  Refuse it instead, matching `bus_pci_data_access''s own
	    empty-slot contract (bus_pci.c, the `dev == NULL' arm: all-ones at register
	    0, zero elsewhere) so the guest sees the ordinary "nothing there" answer.
	    Writes are discarded, which leaves backend state untouched.  */
	if (type1 && bus == 0) {		/*  #515  */
		odata = reg == 0? (uint64_t) -1 : 0;
	} else {
		/*  Pass PCI accesses onto bus_pci:  */
		bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pci_data, bus, dev, func, reg);
		bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pci_data, writeflag == MEM_READ?
		    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
{
	unsigned int ofs, i;
	uint8_t byte;

	relative_addr >>= 5;

	/*
	 *  #530: the byte for ISA port P sits at buffer position (P & 3) % len
	 *  on BOTH arms -- the lane for the 21066's own len 4/8 accesses, and
	 *  the rule the write arm already applied wherever the old guard let
	 *  it run.  That guard's `ofs > len' exit(1) ended the host on a guest
	 *  byte access to a port 2 or 3 mod 4 or a word access to a port 3 mod
	 *  4 (ungated BWX; and at a port 3 mod 4 also the 1- or 2-byte head
	 *  of an unaligned load or store that memory_rw splits at 1 KB),
	 *  and the read arm discarded the
	 *  fetched byte at ofs == len.
	 *  One byte is forwarded whatever len is (the size field a<4:3> is
	 *  not decoded here).  len 0 has no producer; refuse it before the %.
	 */
	if (len == 0)
		return 0;
	ofs = (relative_addr & 3) % len;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
		byte = data[ofs];
		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
	}

	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);

	for (i=0; i<len; i++)
		data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;

	return 1;
}


/*
 *  #518 (lcabadval): one bounded announce per (device instance, reason).  Six
 *  IOC write arms used to end the host with fatal()+exit(1) on any value outside
 *  a tiny accepted set -- and the sets were WRONG, not merely harsh: the 21066
 *  HRM (Table 6-6) lists THIRTEEN legal W_MASK encodings where the model took
 *  one, and S6.4.14 REQUIRES SROM to write ZERO to W_BASE1, which was fatal.
 *  The arms below store the ARCHITECTED FIELDS, ignore reserved bits (type X),
 *  validate W_MASK by its field rule, and decline what the model cannot honour
 *  with this announce -- fatal() prints and returns (it cannot be silenced by
 *  -q, which is why a latch and not a verbosity flag).
 */
static void lca_ioc_announce(struct lca_data *d, int bit, const char *what,
	uint64_t v)
{
	if (d->warned & (1u << bit))
		return;
	d->warned |= 1u << bit;
	fatal("[ lca_ioc: %s: 0x%" PRIx64 "  (once) ]\n", what, v);
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)
{
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	switch (relative_addr + LCA_IOC_BASE) {

	case LCA_IOC_BASE:
		/*  Ignore? Linux reads from the base at startup.  */
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_CONF:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->ioc_conf;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was `if (idata & ~1) { fatal(); exit(1); }' with the
			    store BEFORE the check (#506 had widened its diagnostic).
			    HRM S6.2.9: IOC_CONF<1:0> is the CFG AD<1:0> field, and
			    bit 0 selects the configuration cycle TYPE; everything
			    above is reserved and is IGNORED, not fatal.  Bit 0
			    selects type-1 cycles and is what lca_pci_conf
			    reads (#515); bit 1 selects the CFG AD<1:0> encodings 10/11,
			    which are not configuration-cycle types -- stored and
			    announced once, not modelled.  (Pass 2: this comment first
			    called bit 1 the type-1 select; grok read lca_pci_conf.)  */
			d->ioc_conf = idata & 0x3ULL;
			if (idata & 0x2ULL)
				lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_CONF_CFG1, "IOC_CONF<1> set"
				    " (CFG AD<1:0> = 10/11, not a configuration-cycle type):"
				    " stored, not modelled", idata);
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_TBIA:
		/*  TLB Invalidate All.  */
		/*  TODO: For now, let's just ignore it.  */
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_TB_ENA:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->tlb_enable;
		} else {
			/*  #518: TEN is the only architected bit (HRM Table 6-13);
			    the rest are type X -- IGNORED on write, never fatal.  The
			    old arm stored the whole value and then died on it.  */
			d->tlb_enable = idata & IOC_TB_ENA_TEN;
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_BASE0:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_base_0;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was a two-value whitelist + exit(1).  The HRM defines
			    this register by FIELDS -- W_BASE<31:20>, SG<32>, WEN<33> --
			    not by a list of numbers, and S6.4.14 REQUIRES SROM to write
			    zero here.  Store the fields (reserved bits dropped); the
			    window is not translated by this model, which is announced
			    once when any window is ENABLED.  */
			d->window_base_0 = idata & (IOC_W_BASE_W_BASE | IOC_W_BASE_SG | IOC_W_BASE_WEN);
			if (idata & IOC_W_BASE_WEN)
				lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WEN, "a DMA window was"
				    " enabled; window translation is not modelled", idata);
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_MASK0:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_mask_0;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was `!= 0x700000ULL' + exit(1): ONE accepted value where
			    HRM Table 6-6 lists THIRTEEN (W_MASK<31:20> = 0, 1, 3, 7, ...
			    0xfff: a contiguous low-ones field), and the old arm STORED
			    before it checked.  Validate the field, store only when it is
			    legal, decline once otherwise with the previous value kept.  */
			{
				uint64_t v = idata & 0xfff00000ULL, f = v >> 20;
				if ((f & (f + 1)) == 0)
					d->window_mask_0 = v;
				else
					lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WMASK0, "unsupported"
					    " IOC_W_MASK0 encoding declined (HRM Table 6-6);"
					    " previous value kept", idata);
			}
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_T_BASE0:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_t_base_0;
		} else {
			d->window_t_base_0 = idata;
			/*  TODO: Actually implement this.  */
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_BASE1:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_base_1;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was a ONE-value whitelist + exit(1) -- and the one value
			    was not zero, which S6.4.14 item 3 requires SROM to write.
			    Same disposition as W_BASE0 above.  */
			d->window_base_1 = idata & (IOC_W_BASE_W_BASE | IOC_W_BASE_SG | IOC_W_BASE_WEN);
			if (idata & IOC_W_BASE_WEN)
				lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WEN, "a DMA window was"
				    " enabled; window translation is not modelled", idata);
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_MASK1:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_mask_1;
		} else {
			/*  #518: was `!= 0x3ff00000ULL' + exit(1): ONE accepted value where
			    HRM Table 6-6 lists THIRTEEN (W_MASK<31:20> = 0, 1, 3, 7, ...
			    0xfff: a contiguous low-ones field), and the old arm STORED
			    before it checked.  Validate the field, store only when it is
			    legal, decline once otherwise with the previous value kept.  */
			{
				uint64_t v = idata & 0xfff00000ULL, f = v >> 20;
				if ((f & (f + 1)) == 0)
					d->window_mask_1 = v;
				else
					lca_ioc_announce(d, LCA_W_IOC_WMASK1, "unsupported"
					    " IOC_W_MASK1 encoding declined (HRM Table 6-6);"
					    " previous value kept", idata);
			}
		}
		break;

	case LCA_IOC_W_T_BASE1:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			odata = d->window_t_base_1;
		} else {
			d->window_t_base_1 = idata;
			/*  TODO: Actually implement this.  */
		}
		break;

	default:
		/*  #518: was fatal()+exit(1) for ANY offset the switch does not
		    list.  Decline the write (no IOC register changes; only the announce
		    latch moves), answer a read with
		    zero (odata is already 0), announce each direction once.  The
		    value keeps #506's full width.  NOT all of these offsets are
		    unimplemented HOLES: STAT0 (+0x040), STAT1 (+0x060), SFT_RST
		    (+0x0c0) and PAR_DIS (+0x0e0) are DOCUMENTED registers this
		    model has never had, and S6.4.14 items 1-2 require SROM to
		    write SFT_RST and STAT0 -- those now survive as declined writes
		    instead of ending the process, and are filed (lcaiocstat0) for
		    an implementation, which this decline is not.  (grok, pass 2)  */
		{
			int bit = writeflag == MEM_WRITE ?
			    LCA_W_IOC_DEF_W : LCA_W_IOC_DEF_R;
			if (!(d->warned & (1u << bit))) {
				d->warned |= 1u << bit;
				fatal("[ lca_ioc: unimplemented %s to offset 0x%x",
				    writeflag == MEM_WRITE? "write" : "read", (int)
				    relative_addr);
				if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
					fatal(": 0x%" PRIx64 " -- declined",
					    (uint64_t)idata);	/*  #506  */
				else
					fatal(" -- answered with zero");
				fatal("  (once per direction) ]\n");
			}
		}
		break;
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}


DEVINIT(lca)
{
	char *interrupt_path;
	struct interrupt interrupt_template;
	struct lca_data *d;

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct lca_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct lca_data)));
	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct lca_data));

	/*  Register a PCI bus:  */
	d->pci_data = bus_pci_init(
	    devinit->machine,
	    "TODO: irq"		/*  pciirq: TODO  */,
	    LCA_PCI_SIO,	/*  pci device io offset  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  pci device mem offset: TODO  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI portbase: TODO  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI membase: TODO  */
	    "TODO: pci irq base",	/*  PCI irqbase: TODO  */
	    LCA_ISA_BASE,	/*  ISA portbase  */
	    LCA_ISA_MEMBASE,	/*  ISA membase  */
	    "TODO: irqbase isa");                /*  ISA irqbase: TODO  */

	/*  Add the "sio0" controller (as seen by NetBSD):  */
	bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pci_data, devinit->machine->memory,
	    0, 7, 0, "i82378zb");

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_pci_conf",
	    LCA_PCI_CONF, 0x20000000, dev_lca_pci_conf_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_isa",
	    LCA_PCI_SIO, 0x10000 << 5, dev_lca_isa_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_ioc",
	    LCA_IOC_BASE, 0x20000000, dev_lca_ioc_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(interrupt_path = (char *)
	    malloc(strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10));
	snprintf(interrupt_path, strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10,
	    "%s.lca", devinit->machine->path);

	memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
	interrupt_template.line = 0;
	interrupt_template.name = interrupt_path;
	interrupt_template.extra = d;
	interrupt_template.interrupt_assert = lca_interrupt_assert;
	interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert = lca_interrupt_deassert;
	interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);

	bus_isa_init(devinit->machine, interrupt_path,
	    BUS_ISA_IDE0 | BUS_ISA_IDE1, LCA_ISA_BASE, LCA_ISA_MEMBASE);

	return 1;
}

/*

#include <stdio.h>

/*
 *  isa_interrupt_common():
 */
void isa_interrupt_common(struct bus_isa_data *d, int old_isa_assert)
{
	int new_isa_assert, x;

	/*  Any interrupt assertions on PIC2 go to irq 2 on PIC1  */
	/*  (TODO: don't hardcode this here)  */
	if (d->pic2->irr & ~d->pic2->ier)
		d->pic1->irr |= 0x04;
	else
		d->pic1->irr &= ~0x04;

	/*  printf("ISA: irr=%02x%02x ier=%02x%02x\n",
	    d->pic2->irr, d->pic1->irr, d->pic2->ier, d->pic1->ier);  */

	new_isa_assert = d->pic1->irr & ~d->pic1->ier;

	if (old_isa_assert == new_isa_assert)
		return;

	if (!new_isa_assert) {
		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		return;
	}

	for (x=0; x<16; x++) {
		if (x == 2)
			continue;

		if (x < 8 && (d->pic1->irr & ~d->pic1->ier & (1 << x)))
			break;

		if (x >= 8 && (d->pic2->irr & ~d->pic2->ier & (1 << (x&7))))
			break;
	}

	*d->ptr_to_last_int = x;

	INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
}


/*
 *  isa_interrupt_assert():
 *
 *  Called whenever an ISA device asserts an interrupt (0..15).
 */
void isa_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct bus_isa_data *d = (struct bus_isa_data *) interrupt->extra;
	int old_isa_assert, line = interrupt->line;
	int mask = 1 << (line & 7);

	old_isa_assert = d->pic1->irr & ~d->pic1->ier;

	if (line < 8)
		d->pic1->irr |= mask;
	else if (d->pic2 != NULL)
		d->pic2->irr |= mask;

	isa_interrupt_common(d, old_isa_assert);
}


/*
 *  isa_interrupt_deassert():
 *
 *  Called whenever an ISA device deasserts an interrupt (0..15).
 */
void isa_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct bus_isa_data *d = (struct bus_isa_data *) interrupt->extra;
	int line = interrupt->line, mask = 1 << (line & 7);
	int old_irr1 = d->pic1->irr, old_isa_assert;

	old_isa_assert = old_irr1 & ~d->pic1->ier;

	if (line < 8)
		d->pic1->irr &= ~mask;
	else if (d->pic2 != NULL)
		d->pic2->irr &= ~mask;

	/*  If IRQ 0 has been cleared, then this is a timer interrupt.
	    Let's ack it here:  */
	if (old_irr1 & 1 && !(d->pic1->irr & 1) &&
	    d->ptr_to_pending_timer_interrupts != NULL &&
            (*d->ptr_to_pending_timer_interrupts) > 0)
                (*d->ptr_to_pending_timer_interrupts) --;

	isa_interrupt_common(d, old_isa_assert);
}


/*
 *  bus_isa_init():
 *
 *  Flags are zero or more of the following, ORed together:
 *
 *  BUS_ISA_EXTERNAL_PIC	Don't register/use isa_interrupt_*().
 *  BUS_ISA_IDE0		Include wdc0.
 *  BUS_ISA_IDE1		Include wdc1.
 *  BUS_ISA_FDC			Include a floppy controller. (Dummy.)
 *  BUS_ISA_VGA			Include old-style (non-PCI) VGA. (*1)
 *  BUS_ISA_VGA_FORCE		Include VGA even when running without X11. (*2)
 *  BUS_ISA_PCKBC_FORCE_USE	Always assume keyboard console, not serial. (*3)
 *  BUS_ISA_PCKBC_NONPCSTYLE	Don't set the pc-style flag for the keyboard.
 *  BUS_ISA_NO_SECOND_PIC	Only useful for 8086 XT (pre-AT) emulation. :-)
 *  BUS_ISA_LPTBASE_3BC		Set lptbase to 0x3bc instead of 0x378.
 *
 *  (*1) For machines with a PCI bus, this flag should not be used. Instead, a
 *       PCI VGA card should be added to the PCI bus.
 *
 *  (*2) For machines where it is easy to select VGA vs serial console during
 *       boot, this flag should not be used. Machines that "always" boot up
 *       in VGA console mode should have it set.
 *
 *  (*3) Similar to *2 above; machines that always boot up with VGA console
 *       should have this flag set, so that the keyboard is always used.
 *
 *  The interrupt_base_path is the name of the bus, CPU, or controller onto
 *  which this ISA bus will be attached, e.g. "machine[0].lca" or
 *  "machine[0].cpu[0].pic1".
 */
struct bus_isa_data *bus_isa_init(struct machine *machine,
	char *interrupt_base_path, uint32_t bus_isa_flags,
	uint64_t isa_portbase, uint64_t isa_membase)
{
	struct bus_isa_data *d;
	char tmpstr[300], tmpstr2[300];
	int wdc0_irq = 14, wdc1_irq = 15;
	int i, tmp_handle, kbd_in_use;
	int lptbase = 0x378;

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct bus_isa_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct bus_isa_data)));
	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct bus_isa_data));

	d->isa_portbase = isa_portbase;
	d->isa_membase  = isa_membase;

	if (!(bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_EXTERNAL_PIC)) {
		/*  Connect to the interrupt which we're interrupting
		    at (usually a CPU):  */
		INTERRUPT_CONNECT(interrupt_base_path, d->irq);

		/*  Register the 16 possible ISA interrupts:  */
		for (i=0; i<16; i++) {
			struct interrupt templ;
			char name[300];
			snprintf(name, sizeof(name),
			    "%s.isa.%i", interrupt_base_path, i);
			memset(&templ, 0, sizeof(templ));
			templ.line = i;
			templ.name = name;
			templ.extra = d;
			templ.interrupt_assert = isa_interrupt_assert;
			templ.interrupt_deassert = isa_interrupt_deassert;
			interrupt_handler_register(&templ);
		}
	}

	kbd_in_use = ((bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_PCKBC_FORCE_USE) ||
	    (machine->x11_md.in_use))? 1 : 0;

	if (machine->machine_type == MACHINE_PREP) {
		/*  PReP with obio controller has both WDCs on irq 13!  */
		wdc0_irq = wdc1_irq = 13;
	}

	if (!(bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_EXTERNAL_PIC)) {
		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "8259 irq=%s addr=0x%llx",
		    interrupt_base_path, (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x20));
		d->pic1 = machine->isa_pic_data.pic1 = (struct pic8259_data *)
		    device_add(machine, tmpstr);
		d->ptr_to_pending_timer_interrupts =
		    machine->isa_pic_data.pending_timer_interrupts;
		d->ptr_to_last_int = &machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;

		if (bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_NO_SECOND_PIC)
			bus_isa_flags &= ~BUS_ISA_NO_SECOND_PIC;
		else {
			snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr),
			    "8259 irq=%s.isa.2 addr=0x%llx",
			    interrupt_base_path,(long long)(isa_portbase+0xa0));
			d->pic2 = machine->isa_pic_data.pic2 = (struct pic8259_data *)
			    device_add(machine, tmpstr);
		}
	} else {
		bus_isa_flags &= ~BUS_ISA_EXTERNAL_PIC;
	}

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "8253 irq=%s.isa.%i addr=0x%llx "
	    "in_use=0", interrupt_base_path, 0,
	    (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x40));
	device_add(machine, tmpstr);

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "pccmos irq=%s.isa.%i addr=0x%llx",
	    interrupt_base_path, 8, (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x70));
	device_add(machine, tmpstr);

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "ns16550 irq=%s.isa.%i addr=0x%llx "
	    "name2=tty0 in_use=%i", interrupt_base_path, 4,
	    (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x3f8), 1 - kbd_in_use);
	machine->main_console_handle = (size_t)device_add(machine, tmpstr);

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "ns16550 irq=%s.isa.%i addr=0x%llx "
	    "name2=tty1 in_use=0", interrupt_base_path, 3,
	    (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x2f8));
	device_add(machine, tmpstr);

	if (bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_LPTBASE_3BC) {
		bus_isa_flags &= ~BUS_ISA_LPTBASE_3BC;
		lptbase = 0x3bc;
	}

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "lpt irq=%s.isa.%i addr=0x%llx "
	    "name2=lpt in_use=0", interrupt_base_path, 7,
	    (long long)(isa_portbase + lptbase));
	device_add(machine, tmpstr);

	if (bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_IDE0) {
		bus_isa_flags &= ~BUS_ISA_IDE0;
		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "wdc irq=%s.isa.%i "
		    "addr=0x%llx", interrupt_base_path, wdc0_irq,
		    (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x1f0));
		if (diskimage_exist(machine, 0, DISKIMAGE_IDE) ||
		    diskimage_exist(machine, 1, DISKIMAGE_IDE))
			device_add(machine, tmpstr);
	}

	if (bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_IDE1) {
		bus_isa_flags &= ~BUS_ISA_IDE1;
		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "wdc irq=%s.isa.%i "
		    "addr=0x%llx", interrupt_base_path, wdc1_irq,
		    (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x170));
		if (diskimage_exist(machine, 2, DISKIMAGE_IDE) ||
		    diskimage_exist(machine, 3, DISKIMAGE_IDE))
			device_add(machine, tmpstr);
	}

	if (bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_FDC) {
		bus_isa_flags &= ~BUS_ISA_FDC;
		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "fdc irq=%s.isa.%i "
		    "addr=0x%llx", interrupt_base_path, 6,
		    (long long)(isa_portbase + 0x3f0));
		device_add(machine, tmpstr);
	}

	if (bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_VGA) {
		if (machine->x11_md.in_use || bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_VGA_FORCE)
			dev_vga_init(machine, machine->memory,
			    isa_membase + 0xa0000, isa_portbase + 0x3c0,
			    machine->machine_name);
		bus_isa_flags &= ~(BUS_ISA_VGA | BUS_ISA_VGA_FORCE);
	}

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "%s.isa.1", interrupt_base_path);
	snprintf(tmpstr2, sizeof(tmpstr2), "%s.isa.12", interrupt_base_path);
	tmp_handle = dev_pckbc_init(machine, machine->memory,
	    isa_portbase + 0x60, PCKBC_8042, tmpstr, tmpstr2,
	    kbd_in_use, bus_isa_flags & BUS_ISA_PCKBC_NONPCSTYLE? 0 : 1);

	if (kbd_in_use)
		machine->main_console_handle = tmp_handle;

	bus_isa_flags &= ~(BUS_ISA_PCKBC_NONPCSTYLE | BUS_ISA_PCKBC_FORCE_USE);

	if (bus_isa_flags != 0)
		fatal("WARNING! bus_isa(): unimplemented bus_isa_flags 0x%x\n",
		    bus_isa_flags);

	return d;
}


[32m succeeded in 177ms:[0m
/*
 *  Copyright (C) 2005-2021  Anders Gavare.  All rights reserved.
 *
 *  Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without
 *  modification, are permitted provided that the following conditions are met:
 *
 *  1. Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright
 *     notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.
 *  2. Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright  
 *     notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer in the 
 *     documentation and/or other materials provided with the distribution.
 *  3. The name of the author may not be used to endorse or promote products
 *     derived from this software without specific prior written permission.
 *
 *  THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE AUTHOR AND CONTRIBUTORS ``AS IS'' AND
 *  ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, THE
 *  IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE
 *  ARE DISCLAIMED.  IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE   
 *  FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL, SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR CONSEQUENTIAL
 *  DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF SUBSTITUTE GOODS
 *  OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS INTERRUPTION)
 *  HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN CONTRACT, STRICT
 *  LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE) ARISING IN ANY WAY
 *  OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF
 *  SUCH DAMAGE.
 *   
 *
 *  COMMENT: DEC Alpha machines
 */

#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdlib.h>

#include "cpu.h"
#include "device.h"
#include "devices.h"
#include "machine.h"
#include "memory.h"
#include "misc.h"

#include "thirdparty/alpha_autoconf.h"
#include "thirdparty/alpha_rpb.h"


MACHINE_SETUP(alpha)
{
	struct rpb rpb;
	struct crb crb;
	struct ctb ctb;
	struct mddt mddt;
	struct pcs *pcs = (struct pcs *) malloc(sizeof(struct pcs) * machine->ncpus);
	int i;

	switch (machine->machine_subtype) {

	case ST_ALPHABOOK1:
		machine->machine_name = strdup("AlphaBook 1");
		if (machine->emulated_hz == 0)
			machine->emulated_hz = 233000000;
		device_add(machine, "lca");
		break;

	case ST_DEC_4100:
		machine->machine_name = strdup("AlphaServer 4100");
		break;

	case ST_DEC_3000_300:
		machine->machine_name = strdup("DEC 3000/300");
		machine->main_console_handle = (size_t)device_add(machine,
		    "z8530 addr=0x1b0200000 irq=0 addr_mult=4");
		break;

	case ST_EB164:
		machine->machine_name = strdup("EB164");
		break;

	default:fatal("Unimplemented Alpha machine type %i\n",
		    machine->machine_subtype);
		exit(1);
	}

	if (!machine->prom_emulation)
		return;

	/*  These are used by NetBSD/alpha:  */
	/*  a0 = First free Page Frame Number  */
	/*  a1 = PFN of current Level 1 page table  */
	/*  a2 = Bootinfo magic  */
	/*  a3 = Bootinfo pointer  */
	/*  a4 = Bootinfo version  */
	cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_A0] = 16*1024*1024 / 8192;
	cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_A1] = 0;	/*  TODO  */
	cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_A2] = 0;	/*  Note: NOT ALPHA_BOOTINFO_MAGIC  */
	cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_A3] = 0;	/*  TODO  */
	cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_A4] = 1;

	/*
	 *  HWRPB: Hardware Restart Parameter Block
	 *
	 *  TODO: Almost everything.
	 */
	memset(&rpb, 0, sizeof(struct rpb));
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_phys), 0x14000);
	strlcpy((char *)&(rpb.rpb_magic), "HWRPB", 8);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_version), HWRPB_DSRDB_MINVERS);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_size), sizeof(struct rpb));
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_page_size), 8192);
	strlcpy((char *)&(rpb.rpb_ssn), "123456789", 10);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_type), machine->machine_subtype);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_cc_freq), machine->emulated_hz);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_intr_freq), 1024 << 12);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_pcs_cnt), machine->ncpus);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_pcs_size), sizeof(struct pcs));
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_pcs_off), PCS_ADDR - HWRPB_ADDR);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_ctb_off), CTB_ADDR - HWRPB_ADDR);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_crb_off), CRB_ADDR - HWRPB_ADDR);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(rpb.rpb_memdat_off), MEMDAT_ADDR - HWRPB_ADDR);

	/*  CTB: Console Terminal Block  */
	memset(&ctb, 0, sizeof(struct ctb));
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ctb.ctb_term_type), machine->x11_md.in_use?
	    CTB_GRAPHICS : CTB_PRINTERPORT);

	/*  CRB: Console Routine Block  */
	memset(&crb, 0, sizeof(struct crb));
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(crb.crb_v_dispatch), CRB_ADDR - 0x100);
	store_64bit_word(cpu, CRB_ADDR - 0x100 + 8, PROM_ENTRY_PADDR);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(crb.crb_v_fixup), CRB_ADDR - 0x80);
	store_64bit_word(cpu, CRB_ADDR - 0x80 + 8, PROM_ENTRY_PADDR + 0x800);

	/*  PCS: Processor ID etc.  */
	for (i=0; i<machine->ncpus; i++) {
		memset(&pcs[i], 0, sizeof(struct pcs));
		store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
		    &(pcs[i].pcs_flags), PCS_RC | PCS_PA | PCS_PP |
		    PCS_CV | PCS_PV | PCS_PMV | PCS_PL);
		store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
		    &(pcs[i].pcs_proc_type),
		    machine->cpus[i]->cd.alpha.cpu_type.pcs_type);
	}

	/*
	 *  MDDT: Memory Data Descriptor Table. For now, it is a simple
	 *  two-entry table with half of the available RAM in each entry.
	 *  (The values are in number of 8K pages.)
	 *  The first 16 MB are not included (the kernel lives there).
	 *  The last 1 MB is not included either, it is reserved for bootup
	 *  and similar.
	 */
	memset(&mddt, 0, sizeof(struct mddt));
	memset(&mddt.mddt_clusters[0], 0, sizeof(struct mddt_cluster));
	memset(&mddt.mddt_clusters[1], 0, sizeof(struct mddt_cluster));
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(mddt.mddt_cluster_cnt), 2);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(mddt.mddt_clusters[0].mddt_pfn), 16 * 128);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(mddt.mddt_clusters[0].mddt_pg_cnt),
	    (machine->physical_ram_in_mb/2 - 16) * 128);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(mddt.mddt_clusters[1].mddt_pfn),
	    machine->physical_ram_in_mb/2 * 128);
	store_64bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(mddt.mddt_clusters[1].mddt_pg_cnt),
	    (machine->physical_ram_in_mb/2) * 128);

	/*
	 *  Place a special "hack" palcode call at PROM_ENTRY_PADDR and
	 *  PROM_ENTRY_PADDR + 0x800:
	 *  (Hopefully nothing else will be there.)
	 */
	store_32bit_word(cpu, PROM_ENTRY_PADDR, 0x3fffffe);
	store_32bit_word(cpu, PROM_ENTRY_PADDR + 0x800, 0x3fffffd);

	store_buf(cpu, HWRPB_ADDR, (char *)&rpb, sizeof(struct rpb));
	store_buf(cpu, CTB_ADDR, (char *)&ctb, sizeof(struct ctb));
	store_buf(cpu, CRB_ADDR, (char *)&crb, sizeof(struct crb));
	store_buf(cpu, MEMDAT_ADDR, (char *)&mddt, sizeof(struct mddt));
	store_buf(cpu, PCS_ADDR, (char *)pcs, sizeof(struct pcs) *
	    machine->ncpus);

	free(pcs);
}


MACHINE_DEFAULT_CPU(alpha)
{
	switch (machine->machine_subtype) {

	case ST_ALPHABOOK1:
		machine->cpu_name = strdup("21066");
		break;

	case ST_DEC_4100:
		machine->cpu_name = strdup("21164A-2");
		break;

	case ST_DEC_3000_300:
		machine->cpu_name = strdup("21064");
		break;

	case ST_EB164:
		machine->cpu_name = strdup("21164PC");
		break;

	default:fatal("Unimplemented Alpha machine type %i\n",
		    machine->machine_subtype);
		exit(1);
	}
}


MACHINE_DEFAULT_RAM(alpha)
{
	machine->physical_ram_in_mb = 128;
}


MACHINE_REGISTER(alpha)
{
	MR_DEFAULT(alpha, "Alpha", MACHINE_ALPHA);

	machine_entry_add_alias(me, "alpha");

	machine_entry_add_subtype(me, "AlphaBook 1", ST_ALPHABOOK1,
	    "alphabook1", NULL);

	machine_entry_add_subtype(me, "AlphaServer 4100", ST_DEC_4100,
	    "alphaserver4100", NULL);

	machine_entry_add_subtype(me, "DEC 3000/300", ST_DEC_3000_300,
	    "3000/300", NULL);

	machine_entry_add_subtype(me, "EB164", ST_EB164,
	    "eb164", NULL);

	me->set_default_ram = machine_default_ram_alpha;
}

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "cpu.h"
